2467. Write an example exchange between the hero and the partner. => TUT: "You didn't move. When the man with the knife came. Everyone moves."
KAEL: "You didn't move either."
TUT: "I'm used to it."
KAEL: "……"
TUT: "That's the third this season. Your turn."
KAEL (moving his senet piece with a hand that won't stop shaking): "Pharaoh. Who sent him?"
TUT: "Someone who loves me very much, probably. They all say they do."
2468. Write a short exchange between the hero and the villain. => AMENHOTEP: "Who taught you to fear that cup, little one?"
KAEL: "My mother said wine makes boys sick, my lord."
AMENHOTEP: "Did she. A wise woman. Where is she now?"
KAEL: "Far away."
AMENHOTEP (smiling, setting the cup down): "Everyone is far away from someone. Be careful, child. Clumsy boys fall down stairs."
2469. Invented terms readers need to learn, with meanings => Kemet: the Egyptians' name for Egypt, the Black Land; Deben: a bronze weight used as money; Ma'at: truth, order and justice, what a pharaoh must uphold; Duat: the underworld; Senet: the board game of passing; Ka: a person's life force; Ba: the part of the soul that travels; Uraeus: the rearing cobra on the royal crown; Khopesh: a curved bronze sword; Vizier: the pharaoh's highest official; The Nile Pact: the team's oath, 'What flows returns'

Kael's "Kael" is the only name that sounds foreign there, and people remark on it. No named special moves.

2494. Write the hero's inner monologue at the climax. => *Steel. Lamplight on steel. Tut's face, mouth open. I'm already moving. Of course I am. Eighteen years of fencing drills and a ten-year-old's legs. Too slow. Not too slow. The blade goes in under my ribs, and it's cold. Strange. I thought it would be hot. Tut is screaming. Good. Screaming means breathing. The man pulls the blade out, and the cold turns to fire. Get up. Get UP. My legs won't. They see a child. Tonight they're right. I am a child, bleeding on the floor of a king's room three thousand years before I was born, and nobody is coming. Then the ceiling breaks.*

2554. How should the real timeline of Tutankhamun's reign be handled? => Follow it closely

2556. Books, documentaries or sources you'd like me to lean on => Ian Shaw's 'The Oxford History of Ancient Egypt' for political structure, dynastic context, and the broader Eighteenth Dynasty backdrop.; Joyce Tyldesley's 'Tutankhamun: The Life and Times' for a focused account of the boy-king's reign and court.; Nicholas Reeves' 'The Complete Tutankhamun' for tomb evidence, material culture, and the Amarna-era aftermath shaping Kemet's politics.; Documentaries on Egyptian Bronze Age warfare and daily life, plus Egyptology journals like the Journal of Egyptian Archaeology, for fine-grained, verifiable details.

2710. Should the story cut between two scenes happening at once? => Yes
2711. Which scenes could cut back and forth? => Chapter 4: alternate Kalici lying to Amenhotep about losing her nerve with flashbacks of the night she and Kael talked about her father's death, revealing the truth in pieces.
2712. Describe the rhythm of a perfect chapter: slow, fast, slow? => Open each chapter on a still image of time passing, like the lamp wick in Kael's chamber or the Nile line rising on the temple wall. Keep the first two pages slow. Then move quickly through the conversation, pushing the 1-2 page dialogues to a hard cut at the peak tension. End the chapter on a single striking line, such as Amenhotep's slow smile, so the cliffhanger lands on a page turn.
2713. How clear should action be? => Clear and easy to follow
2714. How should speed be shown? => Let Kael's child-sized speed set the pace. Show distance and seconds counted in heartbeats, so his fast thinking matters more than raw speed.
2715. How should power be shown? => Power shows up as who can open a door, who the guards obey, and who gets served first. Kael reads the room by watching hands, seating, and which rings glint at the table.
2716. How should pain be shown? => Pain unfolds slowly and directly: heartbeats counted on the page, the blade's sting described fully, Kael's breath catching before any scream. Readers feel every second.
2717. Should battles include maps or tactical diagrams? => Yes
2718. Should onlookers react to fights? => Yes
2719. How clear should it be who's winning? => 3
2720. Describe a favorite action scene from a manga or light novel. => The death of the MC in code geass 
2721. How should a single strike be paced across sentences? => Break the strike into one-beat lines. The assassin's arm lifts. One heartbeat. The blade leaves the sleeve. Two heartbeats. Kael is already moving, though his child's legs are slower than his thoughts. Each beat gets its own short line, so the reader counts the seconds with him before the blade lands.

2844. Anything else about how files should be organized? => Inside Volume 1, keep folders for Manuscript, Story Bible, Research, Art and PDFs. Keep old drafts in an "Archive" folder rather than deleting them.
2845. Which PDFs do you want? => A reading copy for you; A proof copy for early readers; A print-ready interior; A cover PDF; A sample chapter for promotion
2846. When should we make the first PDF? => After Chapter 1
2847. What page size should the PDF use? => Light novel paperback (about 5.5 × 8.25 in)

2849. Should the PDF include illustrations, once they exist? => Yes
2850. Should we mark up proof PDFs with comments and highlights for revisions? => Yes

2854. Should separate chapter PDFs be combined into one volume PDF? => Yes
2855. Should the final PDF be compressed for easy sharing? => Yes
2856. Should the print-ready PDF follow a printer's specs (margins, bleed, embedded fonts)? => Yes
2857. Anything else about the PDFs? => Make a reading PDF after each finished chapter so I can read it like a real book, and a combined volume PDF at the end.
2858. Describe the illustration style in one sentence. => Black-and-white seinen realism with gritty, dense cross-hatching, bold screentone shadows, and brutal, detailed battle panels that echo classic war-epic manga.
2859. Style keywords for every illustration => seinen manga; black and white with screentones; detailed Ancient Egyptian architecture; ornate period-accurate clothing; thin, delicate linework; high contrast; dramatic lamplight; Red River-inspired
2860. Things to keep out of every illustration => chibi or cute faces on Kael; sparkly eyes; modern objects in Egypt (except Kael's yen bill and bracelet); fan service; anime-style power effects; text or watermarks in the image
2861. Illustration color mode => Black and white
2862. What should illustrations focus on? => Characters
2863. Aspect ratio for illustrations => Portrait page (2:3)
2864. Which camera angles should the illustrations use often? => Eye level; Low angle (heroic); High angle (vulnerable); Close-up; Wide shot
2865. Should illustrations feel polished or hand-drawn? => 3
2866. Line weight => Varied
2867. Describe the cover illustration in a sentence or two => Kael, small and blond with a scar over one eye, in a blue shirt with red sleeves, stands before a towering gold throne and looks back over his shoulder with cold adult eyes. His shadow is a grown man's.
2868. Describe a quiet-scene illustration in a sentence => Kael and Tutankhamun playing senet by lamplight on the floor of the king's bedchamber, both of them just boys for a moment.
2869. Describe an action-scene illustration in a sentence => Kael throwing himself between a falling blade and the sleeping king as smoke rolls over the bed and lamplight flares off the steel.
2870. Should we make reference sheets (front, side, back, expressions) for each main character first? => Yes
2871. What should each reference sheet show? => Front view; Side view; Back view; Expressions; Outfit details; Weapons and props; Height comparison
2872. Which characters need sheets? => The main cast and the villain
2873. Should each character get a fixed list of visual details (e.g. “blond hair, blue eyes, scar over one eye”)? => Yes
2874. Fixed visual details for the hero => Blond short wavy hair in sharp layers, blue eyes, scar over one eye, 130 cm (looks ten), slim, right-handed, frayed linen bracelet, dagger at his side. Black hair and a black school uniform only in the Japan scenes.
2875. Fixed visual details for the villain => Tall and gaunt, shaved head with gold ornament, hazel eyes, ritual scars, linen-wrapped scarred fingers with many gold rings, indigo robes with gold Amun hieroglyphs, a lapis and carnelian collar.
2876. Should we make a height lineup of the cast? => Yes
2877. Should outfits stay identical across chapters unless the story changes them? => Yes
2878. Which details must never change (the side of a scar, eye color, the dominant hand)? => Kael's blond hair, blue eyes, the side of his eye scar, his height (always a head shorter than Tut's guards' waists) and his right hand. Tut's gold collar. Amenhotep's rings.
2879. Should we make reference images of key locations too? => Yes
2880. Should we make references for props (weapons, artifacts)? => Yes
2881. Which consistency rules do you care about most? => Kael must always look like a ten-year-old next to adults, with eyes that look older than his face. He never gets cute or chibi in the main story. Faces, heights and outfits stay the same in every illustration unless the story changes them.
2882. Hard “never” list: things that must never appear in this story => No sexual or romantic content involving Kael, Tutankhamen, Kalici, or anyone in a child's body; their bonds stay friendship, loyalty, and protection.; No boys' love pairings or romantic subplots between male characters, including Ren, Aneb, Nakhti, and Kael.; No chibi art style, harem setups, overpowered heroes, or shouted technique names; no preachy moralizing from the narration.
2883. Soft limits: things that can appear only if handled with care => Torture; Death of children; Slavery; Animal harm
2884. Describe the most violent moment you're okay with. => The killing of the slave boy in Chapter 8 and the midnight stab wound: graphic and brutal, shown directly.
2885. Is there a swear word or insult that goes too far? => Slurs about race or real-world groups.
2886. Is it okay for the hero to do something truly unforgivable? => Yes
2887. Can animals be hurt on the page? => Yes
2888. Can children be in danger in the story? => Yes
2889. Can main characters be tortured on the page? => Yes
2890. Can real-world religions appear? => Yes
2891. Can real-world politics appear? => No
2892. Can real historical figures appear? => Yes
2893. Fan service? => None
2894. Is drug or alcohol use okay? => Yes
2895. Should topics like suicide or self-harm stay out of the story? => Yes
2896. Should we add content warnings at the start of chapters if needed? => No
2897. Is there content you're nervous about but want to try? => Kael killing an innocent person to protect his secret, and keeping readers on his side afterward.
2898. Should the light novel be safe to show your family? => No
2899. Overall rating target => Mature
2900. Any group or culture we should be especially careful to portray well? => Ancient Egyptians, and Egyptian culture today. Portray them faithfully and with respect, as real people, not exotic props.
2901. Anything else that's off-limits? => Nothing sexual involving anyone under 18 or anyone in a child's body, Kael included. No boys love. No real-world politics.
2902. Rules of the world that can never be broken => No one may be executed until the Weighing of the Heart is performed before the pharaoh, and the vizier cannot skip this rite.
2903. Rules for the hero: things they would never do => Never reveals his true age or origin to anyone outside his inner circle; Never harms Tutankhamun; Never abandons Kalici; Never begs
2904. Rules for the villain => Never harms the pharaoh openly; Never raises his voice; Never acts without a scapegoat ready; Never gets his own hands dirty
2905. Can time travel exist? => Yes
2906. Can the dead come back to life? => No
2907. Can powers solve every problem? => No
2908. Can a character's death be undone? => No
2909. Is luck allowed to help the hero? => Yes
2910. How strict should the logic be? => 8
2911. Should the hero always win in the end? => No
2912. Can good characters do bad things without consequences? => No
2913. Can the villain win sometimes? => Yes
2914. One storytelling rule you insist on => History wins. Real events of Tutankhamun's reign stay accurate, and the story lives in the gaps.
2915. One cliché you forbid => An overpowered hero who's strong from the start.
2916. One thing every chapter must have => A cliffhanger at the end.
2917. Any other rules for the story? => No magic powers. The only supernatural force is the God of the Past and the curse. Kael wins with his mind and with sacrifice, never strength. Every death matters. The romance never happens while Kael is in a child's body.
2918. If your answers contradict each other, I should... => Ask you
2919. What counts as official canon? => The light novel plus this questionnaire
2920. Should I keep a timeline of events? => Yes
2921. Should I track who knows what (a secrets tracker)? => Yes
2922. Should I track each character's injuries and items between scenes? => Yes
2923. Should I flag plot holes when I see them? => Yes
2924. Which detail would annoy you most if it were wrong? => The historical details of Tutankhamun's Egypt.
2925. Name spellings => Strict, never vary
2926. Should there be hidden details for fans to find (Easter eggs)? => Yes
2927. An Easter egg idea => The 10,000 yen bill shows up in the background of a Vizier scene long before anyone mentions it.
2928. Can we change earlier details if a better idea comes along? => Yes
2929. Where should decisions be saved? => Both
2930. Should I remind you of open questions before each chapter? => Yes
2931. Anything else about keeping the story consistent? => When this questionnaire contradicts itself, ask me which answer wins before writing.
2932. What happens in Volume 2, if you know? => I don't know yet
2933. What's the next big arc after Volume 1? => The prison arc: freeing Nakhti from the Vizier's prison while hunting the hooded man and the old king's poisoner.
2934. Who becomes important in Volume 2? => Ren, Wennefer and the hooded man who smells of myrrh and burnt honey.
2935. Which new place appears in Volume 2? => The Vizier's prison beneath the Temple of Amun, and the Valley of the Kings.
2936. Which new power or ability appears later? => None. There are no powers, but Kael learns to fight with a khopesh and to command soldiers.
2937. Who dies later in the series, if anyone? => Nakhtmin, giving his life as the Shield of the Living Pharaoh. Later, Tutankhamun himself.
2938. Who betrays whom later? => Wennefer betrays Kael's secret to the priesthood, under torture.
2939. What's the midpoint of the whole series? => Kael wins the trust of the army, and Tutankhamun takes real power from the Vizier.
2940. What's the darkest moment of the whole series? => The assassination of Tutankhamun.
2941. What's the final battle? => A great battle on the Nile between the Vizier's forces and the pharaoh's loyal army, thousands of men with swords and shields.
2942. Where does the hero end up? => Beside the throne but never on it: the unseen advisor whose name history never recorded.
2943. Where does the villain end up? => Dead, judged and executed for the old king's murder.
2944. What happens to the partner? => Tutankhamun is assassinated. His death is the biggest event of the series, and Kael fails to stop it.
2945. What happens to the rival? => Aneb becomes Kael's sworn right hand and commander of his guard.
2946. What happens to the mentor? => Nakhti survives prison but loses his sight. He becomes Kael's final counselor.
2947. What happens to the love interest? => Kalici, grown, at Kael's side once the curse is broken and he is an adult, with their first kiss.
2948. Big moments you want to reach someday => Freeing Nakhti from prison; Unmasking Ren; The curse breaking; The battle of thousands; Tutankhamun's death; Avenging Tutankhamun; The kiss with Kalici
2949. What should Volume 1 set up for all of this? => Volume 1 needs to plant: the two-year clock and the hourglass, Ren's bronze ring and his link to the God of the Past, the hooded man's scent, the old king's poisoning, Wennefer's suspicion, Kalici's father's records, the yen bill in the Vizier's hands, Aneb's loyalty to Tut, and the Nile Pact.
2950. How should the whole series end? => Open-ended
2951. The final image of the series => Kael, an adult at last, standing in the shadow beside the throne with Tutankhamun's senet piece in his hand.
2952. The final line of the series => "They never saw me. Good."
2953. Does the hero survive the series? => Yes
2954. Does the villain survive? => No
2955. Is the world saved? => Yes
2956. What does the ending cost? => Tutankhamun's life, and Kael's way home. He can never return to Japan.
2957. What changes in the world forever? => The Vizier's shadow rule ends, and Egypt survives its civil war, kept alive by a man no record can explain.
2958. Should there be an epilogue set years later? => Yes
2959. Describe the epilogue. => Years later an archaeologist in modern Japan opens a tomb chamber and finds a frayed linen bracelet beside a 10,000 yen bill sealed in a jar.
2960. How should the last chapter feel? => Bittersweet and haunting.
2961. Describe the ending you'd love most, even if it isn't final. => Tutankhamun dies young, as history says he must, and Kael can't stop it. He doesn't take the crown. He breaks the curse at the cost of ever going home, avenges his friend, and keeps Tut's Egypt alive from the shadows. Finally, as a grown man, he kisses Kalici. In the last chapter he writes the true story on papyrus and seals it in the tomb. Three thousand years later, someone in Japan finds it.
2962. Could there be spin-off stories? => Yes
2963. Which character deserves a spin-off? => Ren
2964. A prequel idea => The night the old pharaoh was poisoned, told from the point of view of young Nakhtmin standing guard.
2965. A short side-story idea => Kalici's father the scribe and the records he hid.
2966. Should there be a comedy spin-off (chibi or school-life version)? => No
2967. A side story set in the world's past => How Amenhotep rose from scribe to Vizier.
2968. A side story centered on the villain => Amenhotep's childhood, when his parents were executed for heresy.
2969. Is the world big enough for many stories? => Yes
2970. A location that deserves its own story => The Flooded Tombs of Amenhotep.
2971. Any other ways the world could grow? => Other outsiders the God of the Past has sent through history. Ren says Kael is not the first.
